# Oatmeal

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="oatmeal-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="oatmeal-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="oatmeal-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Oatmeal in the HMI library has 67 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium, Co and V from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 67 results from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the oatmeal was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>
</section>

## Oatmeal overview

<a id="hmi-ing-004"></a>
<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>
<a id="which-sourcing-or-production-decisions-deserve-priority-on-the-available-evidence"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Oatmeal in the HMI library has 67 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium, Co and V from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in oatmeal, as sold from 19 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 4.85 to 6.14 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, oatmeal, flour or meal had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 3 composites from 2018–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians with the basis not stated ranged from 2 µg/kg (Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) 2013) to 8.6 µg/kg (Dabeka et al. 1995).<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Spungen et al. 2024 reported cadmium at 2.2 µg/kg (mean) in oatmeal, flour or meal from all samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the oatmeal came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as oatmeal

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers oatmeal in 3 forms kept separate in every comparison: oatmeal, flour or meal (49 results), oatmeal, as sold (16 results) and oatmeal, cooked (2 results).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? <div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-oatmeal" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-oatmeal-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of oatmeal, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

dataset methods and interpretation</summary><div data-shared-evidence-content><p>Source summaries retain their original statistic, material, basis and censoring. Each source drawer identifies methods and overlapping samples. No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:oatmeal-4136582d0f6e:564ae16a7079"><summary>HMI oatmeal Pb reference · Oatmeal, flour or meal</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:oatmeal-4136582d0f6e:564ae16a7079"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI oatmeal Pb reference · Oatmeal, flour or meal. Oatmeal, flour or meal; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤3</td><td>≤4</td><td>2–4 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤3</td><td>≤4</td><td>2–4 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>6 reference units; 3 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>Houlihan et al. 2019</td><td>2</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>Spungen et al. 2024</td><td>1</td><td>33%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for oatmeal, flour or meal on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>3 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. Study</a></p><p>Oatmeal, flour or meal · Lead (Pb) · 3 composites.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;4 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>3 composites. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI oatmeal Pb reference · Oatmeal, flour or meal. median as reported: ≤4 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤3; Q3 2–4 (bounded) µg/kg. 3 families. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without FDA Total Diet Study: Limited data.</li><li>Without Houlihan et al. 2019: Limited data.</li><li>Without Spungen et al. 2024: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:oatmeal-4136582d0f6e:564ae16a7079"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:oatmeal-4136582d0f6e:564ae16a7079">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2018–2020</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>1 collection regions</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>median</td><td>0 of 3 detected; HMI median of individual results</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>3 dataset rows, for example Original FDA TSV row 8903 (header = row 1).</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. Any basis conversion used for its HMI comparison is stated separately with the numerical target.</p></details><p><a href="https://www.fda.gov/food/fda-total-diet-study-tds/fda-total-diet-study-tds-results">Open the primary publication</a> · <a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">HMI source record</a></p></div><div data-evidence-content><p class="hmi-dataset-title"><a href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food" class="internal" data-slug="sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Houlihan et al. 2019</a></p><p>Oatmeal, flour or meal · Lead (Pb) · 1 samples.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">2 µg/kg (sample value)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">sample value.</span><p>1 samples. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach oatmeal

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared oatmeal from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

For cadmium, the HMI reference median was lowest for oatmeal, cooked (2.75 µg/kg) and highest for oatmeal, flour or meal (7.8 µg/kg); forms differ in water content, so this compares foods as sold or eaten, not uptake.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The HMI reference for lead in oatmeal, flour or meal pools 6 subgroup units from 3 sampling families, with a median of below 4 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 3 (lower quartile) and between 2 and 4 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For lead in oatmeal, as sold, the reference median was between 4.1 and 5.16 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 2 to between 7.2 and 8.4 µg/kg across 17 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For lead in oatmeal, cooked, the reference median was below 7 µg/kg, with a middle half from below 7 to below 7 µg/kg across 17 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, oatmeal, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (4.85 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (6.14 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in PAHO (2 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (30 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>


<a id="processing-effects"></a>
<a id="ingredient-derivative-risk"></a>
<a id="processing-effects-and-derivatives"></a>
## Processing, cooking and preparation

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

Processed or prepared forms were compared only with unprocessed oatmeal in the same study and group. Oatmeal, cooked (PAHO) had cadmium lower at 3 µg/kg, against 30 µg/kg for the unprocessed form in the same group (oatmeal, as sold).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Forms differ in water content, so a per-kilogram difference is not by itself a measured removal or gain of metal.



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for oatmeal

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing oatmeal for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.4 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

## Evidence review and remaining gaps

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>
<a id="which-primary-regulatory-instruments-apply-to-this-exact-ingredient-form-species-market-and-date"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-035"></a>

Every source record that names oatmeal was decided on its content: 9 occurrence extracted, 9 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Oatmeal FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-oatmeal" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-oatmeal" data-question="What heavy metals are found in oatmeal?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in oatmeal?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium, Co and V in oatmeal, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in oatmeal, as sold from 19 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 4.85 to 6.14 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-types-of-oatmeal-have-the-lowest-levels" data-anchor="which-types-of-oatmeal-have-the-lowest-levels" data-question="Which types of oatmeal have the lowest levels?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which types of oatmeal have the lowest levels?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

For cadmium, the HMI reference median was lowest for oatmeal, cooked (2.75 µg/kg) and highest for oatmeal, flour or meal (7.8 µg/kg); forms differ in water content, so this compares foods as sold or eaten, not uptake.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-oatmeal" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-oatmeal" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in oatmeal?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in oatmeal?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, oatmeal, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (4.85 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (6.14 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in PAHO (2 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (30 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-processing-change-heavy-metals-in-oatmeal" data-anchor="does-processing-change-heavy-metals-in-oatmeal" data-question="Does processing change heavy metals in oatmeal?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does processing change heavy metals in oatmeal?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Only comparisons between forms in the same study are available, not before-and-after measurements of the same oatmeal.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Oatmeal, cooked (PAHO) had cadmium lower at 3 µg/kg, against 30 µg/kg for the unprocessed form in the same group (oatmeal, as sold).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-oatmeal-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-oatmeal-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in oatmeal is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in oatmeal is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 3 results from 3 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Inorganic Arsenic</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in oatmeal, as sold from 4 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 3 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
